# Acceptance stops

Open evidence gaps (see `docs/PLAN.md`, "Acceptance stops"). A stop blocks only
what depends on it.

`Kind` says how the stop is enforced:

- `project`: a provenance fact about the project's inputs. No code path can
  report it at runtime, so there is no test pin; the row (and the PLAN rule it
  cites) is the record.
- `code`: a gap inside the code. It must be reported explicitly at runtime
  (the "Reported by" column names the mechanism) and pinned by a real test
  (the "Pinned by" column), which asserts the report, never a print.

| ID | Kind | Area | Gap | Reported by | Pinned by | Blocks | Cheapest resolution |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| S-011 | code | INI include paths | `Win32Path` (portable PathRemoveFileSpecA / PathAppendA / PathCanonicalizeA, fitted to the real shlwapi: random paths per call plus long ones, zero mismatches inside the domain) refuses forms it does not port: any length, component length or running length of 250-262 (shlwapi truncates and drops trailing separators there in unported ways), UNC `\\server\share`, `\\?\` device paths, doubled separators, and EMBEDDED DRIVE COMPONENTS, i.e. any `:` other than the `X:` prefix at index 1 (`.\C:\..\x.inc` from `parent.ini` is `C:\x.inc` in shlwapi but the port gave `\x.inc`; the generator now builds these and the fuzz audit shows `:` is the only special character that diverges). Retail data has no such include: the all-`*.ini` pre-pass resolves every include with zero errors. | `Win32Path::isSupportedPathForm()` is false, so `TextFile::resolveIncludePath` returns false and the include is a loud "Could not open include" error (lane INI-1) | `Win32Path refuses embedded drive components (S-011)`, `Win32Path refuses forms outside the ported domain`, `Win32Path reproduces the MAX_PATH failure state ...` and the shlwapi oracle test, whose generator builds colon and special-character segments and asserts they are refused (test_win32path.cpp) | mod or map INI includes with such paths | model the embedded-drive and truncation-band rules against the real calls, or call the real shlwapi on Windows builds |
| S-012 | code | Archive mounts | A mounted disk archive is protected from replacement only where the platform can deny other writers: `_WIN32` (MSVC and MinGW, `_wfsopen` with `_SH_DENYWR`). Elsewhere a rename-over is harmless (the open inode is untouched) but an in-place overwrite cannot be prevented, so a live mount would read the new bytes (`ArchiveHandle`, lane INI-1). An advisory `flock` would not close this: it binds only writers that also take the lock, and an arbitrary process overwriting the file does not. | `ArchiveFileSystem::unverifiedIdentity()` returns one `S-012: archive identity unverified ...` line per mounted archive whose handle is not replacement-protected (`ArchiveFile::isIdentityEnforced()`); empty on Windows | `the mount reports S-012 exactly when the platform cannot enforce deny-write` (asserts empty on `_WIN32`, an `S-012:` line elsewhere) and `an archive opened without deny-write is reported as S-012 on every platform` (test_mount_handles.cpp) | mount-identity guarantee on Linux/macOS builds when another process rewrites an archive in place | compare size and mtime (or a content hash) on every read and fail loudly, or copy archives into private storage at mount |

| S-016 | code | Retail oracle (Apt strings) | Retail string formatting allocates through the game's pool allocator (BFME2 0x00ADB160, a global that startup builds; the helper does not). So the harness cannot run (a) Add2 with a string operand (concatenation) and (b) Equals2 of a non-string, non-boolean value against a string, whose getName compare formats the other side; both fault at BFME2 0x00ADB177. The engine's behaviour for these cases has only hand-traced expected values. | `AptHandlers.run` raises `OracleError` at 0xADB177; the comparison tests EXCLUDE exactly those Equals2 cases and assert the exact exclusion counts (`EXCLUDED_EQUALS2`) | `test_stop_s016_string_concatenation_and_string_vs_nonstring_equals2_fault_in_retail` (the faulting Add2 and Equals2 cases, and that the neighbouring supported cases run) and the exact counts asserted by `test_binary_handler_matches_retail_on_edge_and_random_operands[equals2-6/7]` (tools/retail_oracle/test_apt_handlers.py) | claiming external verification of string concatenation and of Equals2 string-vs-number getName compares | build the pool allocator state (the object behind the global read at 0xADB167) in the helper, or hostfn-backed allocator hooks as `prime_asciistring_runtime` does for RW |
| S-017 | code | Apt atof | Retail MSVCR71 atof differs from correct rounding for decimal texts whose exact value lies in [2e-324, DBL_MIN): it returns TWICE the value for exact powers of two there (and 0 for 2^-1023), the exact decimal of 2^-1075 is 0x1 in retail but 0 in the engine (Boolean true vs false), and 2.225073858507201259573821257020768020077017763406988739288377e-308 is 0x000FFFFFFFFFFFFF in retail but 0x0010000000000000 in the engine. The cause inside the CRT is not decoded. | `AptAtofDouble` decides from the exact value of the TEXT (digit comparison against DBL_MIN's exact decimal expansion, floor 2e-324), so it reports even when the engine's rounded result is zero or a normal number: a pending report "... subnormal double range ... | S-110 | code | Map object initial state | A map object's Drawable starts with model condition flags NIGHT / SNOW only (the map's time of day and weather through GameData `ForceModelsToFollowTimeOfDay` / `ForceModelsToFollowWeather`, and the `objectTime` / `objectWeather` keys, RW 0x695EBD-0x695F2F). Everything else object creation sets is not derived: weapon set and veterancy flags, the damage state from `objectInitialHealth` / `objectMaxHPs`, upgrade visuals (`objectUpgradesList`, `objectGrantUpgrade*`), power (`objectPowered`), stance, construction state, `BuildVariations` (a logic RNG pick), the owner colour is the SidesList `playerColor`, not an MP lobby colour, and the Lua `OnCreated` creation hooks: retail sends every full object (and horde member) the event `OnCreated`, and the handler of its AI module's `AILuaEventsList` (`data\scripts\scriptevents.xml` `EventList` / `Inherit` / `EventHandler EventName="OnCreated"`, function bodies in `data\scripts\scripts.lua`) hides sub objects permanently (`RangerFunctions` -> `OnGondorArcherCreated` hides `FireArowTip`, which `SubObjectsUpgrade` shows again when the fire arrow upgrade is granted, so archers draw their fire arrow tips; also `Forged_Blade`, `Glow`, `Hammer1`, `Torch` ...) or grants upgrades. `MapObjectRuntime::build` RUNS them on the real Lua script engine (lane LUA-1): after the object's draw modules exist the object enters the script world (`LuaScriptEngine::objectEnteredWorld`, RW 0x68E31F) and receives `OnCreated` (`dispatchInternal`; RW 0x628882 / 0x6288FA sendObjectCreated binds the Drawable first, then dispatches), and a hide reaches the drawable through `LuaGameHost::drawableShowSubObject` / `drawableShowModule` (a draw module of that tag first, RW 0x6789B4: the matched module takes the request, its placed model is marked `moduleHidden` and not instanced; a matched module that shows no model here is the reported S-124). What runs is decided by the real event registry from the raw `scriptevents.xml` / `scripts.lua` (`CreationScriptData::rawLoaded`), never by the report-only inventory scanner: a one-line function, a redefinition, a handler assigned through a global expression or a computed hide argument run, a handler naming a missing global is retail's silent failure (RW 0x736019), and a scanner failure is only the `[S-110]` line `the report-only inventory scan ... failed`. Inheritance is resolved from the binary: the handler insert (RW 0x733FAF) overwrites an existing handler of the same event, so a child list's `OnCreated` REPLACES the inherited one (17,790 objects ran 35 own handlers; `OnMordorFighterCreated` 2,274, `OnIsengardFighterCreated` 2,866). Still stops: the order register-then-OnCreated is inferred (spec G7); the logic RNG draw `GetGameLogicRandomValue(1, 999)` (GameLogic.cpp line 0x19A7, RW 0x628892..0x6288A5, unconditional) IS made, in `LuaScriptEngine::sendObjectCreated` (the one shared creation function: draw, then the drawable, then register + OnCreated) for every full object, bridge and horde member, hook or not (51,414 draws over the 181 maps; `MapRuntimeReport::creationDraws`; the generator is the one `MapObjectRuntime::setLogicRandom` names, its algorithm and the seed at map start stay S-080, and the drawing order is the object list order, not retail's earlier bridge / wall pass (IsBridge or WALK_ON_TOP_OF_WALL objects are created first): a bridge / wall pass object that follows a normal object makes `MapObjectRuntime::build` report `[S-110] N bridge / wall pass object(s) ... follow a normal object ...` (`MapRuntimeReport::creationOrderDeviations`), because every later logic draw of the map can then differ from retail); `ObjectGrantUpgrade` / `ObjectHasUpgrade` / `ObjectDoSpecialPower` ... are engine callees that do not exist (S-124: `findUpgrade` and the others are reported, the upgrade is not granted, so e.g. `OnTrollCreated`'s rock-throwing upgrade is missing); a template with several AI modules naming lists uses the first; and a script's hide / show of a name no sub object has (7 names, `arrow`, `arrownock`, `icewall`, `moduletag_drawfloor`, `spear`, `v1s`, `v2s`) is ignored as ZH `doHideShowSubObjs` does (the INI's own 7 names `arrow`, `arrownock`, `icewall`, `moduletag_drawfloor`, `spear`, `v1s`, `v2s`, plus every name a hook hides that some model lacks, e.g. `FireArowTip` on 2,554 of the 6,921 archers' hides, `Forged_Blade`, `Glow`; the name `ModuleTag_DrawFloor` is a draw module tag: no retail object has such a floor hidden at map start). | `MapObjectCreation::stopLines()` `[S-110]` in `MapObjectReport::stops` (carried in every `MapObjectBuilder` report) and `MapObjectReport::unportedKeys` (which keys the objects carry) and the creation hook report (`creationHooksScanned`, `creationHookObjects`, `creationHooksByFunction` / `ByTemplate`, `creationHookHides` / `Shows`, in the `MapObjectBuilder` report `objects.creation_hook*`) | `mapobj creation: initial model condition flags ...` and `mapobj creation: every stop line is registered ...` (test_mapobj_creation.cpp); `mapobj retail: the object loop ...` pins the complete `unportedKeys` table and the complete creation hook tables (test_mapobj_retail.cpp); `mapobj creation hooks: ...` (test_mapobj_creation.cpp: the scans and the report on synthetic data), `mapobj runtime: scripts the report-only scanner cannot read still run on the real engine ...`, `mapobj runtime: every full object draws GetGameLogicRandomValue(1, 999, GameLogic.cpp, 0x19A7) before its drawable and OnCreated ...` (call log with and without hooks and a GetRandomNumber handler), `mapobj runtime: a hide of a matched draw module that shows no model here is reported S-124 ...`, `mapobj runtime: a bridge / wall pass object after a normal object keeps drawable-list order ...` (call-log sequence and the exact S-110 report); `mapobj retail: the object loop ...` also pins 51,414 creation draws | exact appearance of objects that start damaged, upgraded, veteran or unpowered: over the 35,500 full objects of the 181 maps 902 carry `objectInitialHealth` != 100, 3 `objectEnabled = false`, 2 `objectPowered = false`, 142 `objectMaxHPs` / `objectVeterancy`, 558 `objectExperienceLevel`, 2,858 `objectUpgradesList` (counted in `MapObjectReport::unportedKeys`); 17,790 full objects and horde members ran an `OnCreated` handler (35 functions; `OnGondorArcherCreated` 2,262 and `OnRohanArcherCreated` 618 objects show `FireArowTip`; `OnMordorFighterCreated` 5,140, `OnGondorFighterCreated` 3,281 ...; 59 sub objects hidden permanently) | locate the RotWK object creation / Drawable binding code (RW 0x68B53C refreshes the drawable from the object flags at +0x10C) and port the flag setters |

| S-1121 | code | Disconnect path (MP-2) | RotWK's DisconnectManager is ported (update RW 0x8D8A90, updateDisconnectStatus RW 0x8D8735, votes RW 0x8D7F77 / 0x8D8233 / 0x8D826F, processDisconnectFrame RW 0x8D836B with sendFrameDataToPlayer RW 0x8D4761, allOnSameFrame RW 0x8D861A, isLocalPlayerNextPacketRouter RW 0x8D86D0, disconnectPlayer RW 0x8D8553 / 0x8D57D8, the GameData network fields RW 0xC007D0 .. 0xC00850 with the constructor defaults RW 0x6432FF ..). Not located / not ported: the sender of DISCONNECTSCREENOFF (type 29 is inferred from the gap in RW 0x989449; ZH's notifyOthersOfNewFrame on screen off is kept), the GameSpy ping test RW 0x8D7C78 (no pinger in a LAN game: it passes), TheGameLogic + 0x2A4, the left state 1 of ConnectionManager + 0x12080, the filler of the router fallback order (+ 0x12030: here the network slots in slot order), RotWK's frame gate (ZH's screen gate would deadlock a peer that is behind: not applied), the disconnect chat. OpenBFME addition: a peer that announced its disconnect frame F runs F only after a DISCONNECTPLAYER, a later frame of an in-game peer, or the next packet router's release (a DISCONNECTSCREENOFF that lists the announcement), so a drop at F and the late arrival of the leaver's commands for F cannot both happen; two peers that disagree on who the next router is can still race (two failures at once). Inferred (lane MP-2 review): the decider of a drop is the first slot of the lobby's fallback order whose predecessors are all the target or gone, and its DISCONNECTPLAYER names the predecessors it holds gone (a slot mask, OpenBFME's field): a receiver refuses it at once when a predecessor that is not the target is unnamed (judged on the message and the agreed order alone), holds it while a named leave / drop has not been applied here (the frames from the drop frame on wait), and applies it before the drop frame runs, else refuses it (Network::dropClaims / verifyDropClaims); so a membership change never depends on which messages arrived first. With two or more silent slots ahead of every survivor in that order no survivor may decide: the disconnect screen stays until the players Quit | `DisconnectManager::stopLines()` `[S-1121]` (every peer report) | `mp2 disconnect: the lane's acceptance stops are reported (S-1121 .. S-1126)` and the other `mp2 disconnect:` tests (test_mp2_disconnect.cpp) | retail-exact disconnect timing in corner cases, cross-play | read RW 0x8D4E89 / 0x8D2F04 (the command dispatch) for type 29 and the writers of ConnectionManager + 0x12080 / + 0x12030 |

| S-1140 | code | Presentation beyond retail: horde motion (SMOOTH-2) | By the owner's direction (2026-10-06: the infantry and horse battalions "jerked around a lot and seemed to 'lag'") the drawn position is not retail's: RW 0x6765B9's D3DXVec3CatmullRom takes P3 = the pending position, which for a member walking to its slot without a path is the pre-move position (RW 0x5E5B4E), so retail's curve slows to a stop at the end of every logic frame (the drawn speed jumps about 3x at each boundary at 144 fps). `RenderInterpolation::smoothPose` (drawn by `Drawable::syncFromSnapshot`) keeps retail's stale snap and rotation, uses the pending position only as a look-ahead (set in the frame and not the frame's start position), otherwise extrapolates the step, holds a segment without motion, and limits the Hermite tangents (no backward part along the step, at most twice its length). The presented time (`LiveGame::present`) moves continuously, eased towards the clock by at most a quarter of the render step, stops at the end of the latest completed frame while the worker is late, and each late render frame adds 4 ms to the presentation delay (at most +70 ms, decaying 10 ms per second). Presentation only: the simulation and its hash are untouched. Projectiles keep retail's pose. | `[S-1140]` in `DrawableManager::Report::stops` (`RenderInterpolation::stopLines()`) | `smooth2: smoothPose: even steps are linear, a stop holds ...`, `smooth2 retail: hordes and cavalry march, turn, charge ...`, `smooth2 retail: a worker slower than the presentation delay never freezes and jumps ...` (test_smooth2_horde_motion.cpp), `smooth1: the smooth-motion stops are reported ...` (test_smooth_interpolation.cpp) | how close the motion looks to retail at 30 fps | none needed for play; a retail capture of a marching horde at 30 fps shows the pulse this removes |

| S-123 | code | Lua numerics | Script arithmetic (`+ - * /` and the `for` step) is rounded to a 24 bit significand like retail's x87 at precision control 24 (setFPMode RW 0x440809; one rounding of the exact result, checked against the hardware-verified NumericState). That the game runs at PC24 while scripts execute is assumed, not traced; `LUA_EA_NUM_DOUBLE` selects plain doubles. Number text is MSVCR71's %.16g (3 digit exponents, 1.#INF, 1.#QNAN, -1.#IND: CRT facts, the binary only holds the format string RW 0xD0AC04), produced from the exact decimal expansion of the double by an own formatter (no host snprintf / localeconv, independent of the FPU rounding mode and the locale) with MSVCR71's own TWO STAGE rounding (17 digits then 16, each rounded up by its guard digit alone, read from the DLL's instructions `_fltout` 0x7C372A35 / `$I10_OUTPUT` 0x7C37203C / 0x7C355AA0 and run in an x86 emulator; not the correctly rounded text: 1000000000000002.5 prints 1000000000000003, either zero prints `0`; 72,590 sampled doubles agree) and strtod is MSVCR71's (no inf, nan, hex; subnormal results are the host's, S-017 shows MSVCR71 mishandles them); `tonumber(s, base)` is a fixed width 32 bit unsigned strtoul (sign negated mod 2^32, overflow 0xFFFFFFFF, RW 0xB5FA89..0xB5FAE1) and the luaL_check_int/long conversions are `_ftol` (low 32 bits). UNRESOLVED: no running Windows oracle (the DLL values are emulated instruction by instruction), that `$I10_OUTPUT`'s 80 bit power-of-ten table equals the exact expansion for doubles outside the samples, strtod rounding of subnormals and of inputs beyond 17 digits. The string hash and the hashes of every userdata (lua_newuserdata blocks and lua_pushusertag records alike) and table keys are host independent (32 bit hash arithmetic, creation serials; a pointer / tag pair is found by an equality scan), see the README The CRT math functions (sin, cos, pow, fmod, ...), string.format's own exponents and gcinfo() (64 bit sizes) are the host's | `LuaRuntime` reports it for every state it opens | `lua numerics: round24 is one rounding ...`, `lua numerics: PC24 arithmetic agrees with the hardware-verified NumericState ...`, `lua numerics: the VM uses the numeric mode of its state ...`, `lua numerics: tostring of a number is MSVCR71's printf ...`, `lua numerics: the number text is exact and independent of the FPU rounding mode and the locale ...`, `lua numerics: tonumber with a base is MSVCR71's 32 bit unsigned strtoul ...`, `lua numerics: %.16g is MSVCR71's two stage conversion ... pinned against values executed from the DLL's own instructions`, `lua numerics: what stays unresolved is reported ...`, `lua: every host-independent hash ...`, `lua: iteration order does not depend on the heap ...`, `lua: lua_pushusertag userdata are deterministic too: independent processes ...` (test_lua_runtime.cpp, with the probe process tests/lua_userdata_probe.cpp) | scripts that print or compare computed numbers beyond 24 bits, any script using the math library | trace the FPU control word while a handler runs (oracle on Windows); run the math library functions through the oracle |

| S-1540 | code | Lua math library across OSes (WIN-1) | The Lua `math` library functions (`sin`, `cos`, `tan`, `asin`, `acos`, `atan`, `atan2`, `pow`, `log`, `log10`, `exp`, `fmod`; `engine/src/Libraries/Lua/lmathlib.c`) call the host C runtime, whose results differ in the last bit between glibc and the Windows UCRT (lane WIN-1 measured cos / sin / atan2: Wine's UCRT misses the correctly rounded result on 442 of 5,333 inputs, glibc on 12). | S-1541 | project | Windows build (WIN-1) | The Windows x64 build is cross-compiled with llvm-mingw (Clang + libc++ against the UCRT, `engine/cmake/toolchains/windows-x64-llvm-mingw.cmake`, `tools/release/build_windows.sh`) and verified only under Wine 11.19 on Linux: Wine's own ucrtbase, shlwapi, ws2_32, kernel32 and graphics stack stand in for Microsoft's. The doctest suite (2,394 cases), the cross-OS lockstep games (`tools/net/cross_os_lockstep.sh`) and the exported game's boot ran there; no real Windows PC and no MSVC (`build.bat`) build was run. The simulation no longer calls the C runtime's maths or number parsing (sinDD / cosDD / atan2DD, scanRealText), which removes the largest Windows-specific risk, but socket behaviour, file paths, the D3D12 / Vulkan renderer and the shlwapi oracle (`test_win32path.cpp`, skipped under Wine) are Microsoft's on a real PC. | project-level (no runtime report: an environment fact) | n/a (project) | claiming the Windows build ready for public testing without a real-PC run | on a Windows 10 / 11 PC: `build.bat` + `run_tests.bat` (MSVC, x87 oracle, shlwapi oracle), then the exported `OpenBFME.exe` against the retail install, and one LAN game against a Linux peer with `--net-crc` |
| S-1542 | code | INI real-number text (WIN-1) | RESOLVED by lane WIN-1 r2: INI reals are read with MSVCR71's own `sscanf("%f")`, ported instruction by instruction (`Common/INI/Msvcr71Real.h`: `_input` 0x7C36D7CF, `__strgtold12` 0x7C3720A2, `__mtold12` 0x7C37300F, `__multtenpow12` 0x7C37331E, `__ld12mul` 0x7C3730ED, `_ld12cvt` 0x7C3726C3; the power-of-ten tables generated, equal to the DLL's 58 reachable entries), so every OS reads every INI real to retail's bits, the three forms r1 refused included: `3e` reads 3, `0x10` reads the decimal 0, `nan` fails (r1's "measured" UCRT behaviour came from Wine's builtin msvcr71, which Wine loads even for an explicit path: the oracle now verifies the DLL's code bytes). | S-178 | code | Skirmish lobby inferences and the unresolved random-start consumer | (A) RESOLVED BY LANE START-1 (the consumer is GameLogic/NewGame, see S-270; the text below is the contract it implements, with the order confirmed from the RotWK disassembly): the lobby hands over `colour`, `playerTemplate` and `startPos` of -1 for random; `PlayerList::applySkirmishSlots` takes named factions, explicit RGB and start indexes, `LiveGame` applies slots before its logic RNG exists, and only the abstract / recording `NewGameSink` exist. A future skirmish-start lane must resolve them in retail's order (RotWK game.dat 0x62DC3E, S-001 caveat; clean BFME2 1.06 agrees): `populateRandomStartPosition` (0x62D7EA; BFME2 0x64485C) FIRST, then `populateRandomSideAndColor` (0x62D44C; BFME2 0x6444BE); start positions reserve explicit valid ones, then occupied non-observer slots in ascending slot order (random vacant-start branch, team / distance placement with a fixed candidate and tie order), observers after, geometry through the numeric facade; then for each occupied slot in ascending order its faction BEFORE its colour: a random / invalid faction first discards `seed % 7` LogicRandom(0,1) draws (seed accessor 0x6D3204), builds the ordered candidate list for the chosen start and mode and draws LogicRandom(0,1000) % candidateCount with its validity / retry logic, a random colour draws an inclusive index 0..NumColors-1 and rejects occupied colours; explicit valid choices skip their draws; every discard / retry draw, the range reduction and the RNG state must be reproduced and pinned with RNG-call and state golden vectors across peers before a deterministic start is claimed (ZH's side-and-colour-before-start order is wrong here). An adapter must refuse a message whose `SkirmishGameInfo::unresolvedRandomChoices()` is not empty rather than substitute a preferred colour, a null faction or start 0. (B) Inferred lobby contents: the interim setup source scans `playertemplate.ini`, `multiplayer.ini`, `gamedata.ini` by name (LOGIC-1's store replaces it); the faction list is Random plus the store's PlayableSide (+0x151) and not IsObserver (+0x150) templates labelled by DisplayName (RotWK 0x844BD4: no StartingBuilding or side filter) while the mode / map / start and hero restrictions of that routine are not recovered; colours are all configured ones, `AvailableInWotR = No` ones dropped only in strategic mode (flag +0x7C, RotWK 0x8440C7; the human / occupied / observer predicates of that routine are reduced to "another slot holds it"); AI is offered on every map (the +0x11 flag is not in MapCache); no battle-honors stars in the map list and the slot-1 AI is Easy; Hero and Handicap combos stay empty; the rule rows (`MpGameRules::NumCheckBoxes/NumComboBoxes`) and the chat / clan tabs answer 0 and their `InitGadgets` are notes; the ImageComboBox is native BFME code (RotWK 0x7252B6 / 0x7254A7 / 0x4A1EA3) of which the port has the call pattern (20 x 20 image cells AptRandomColor / AptWhiteBox tinted, selected image drawn centred) but not the key and child handling; the map picture is drawn since lane UI-2 (S-1480); the drop-down button's resize is RotWK's since lane UI-2 (RW 0x725688); display names and descriptions fall back to the file name (`$` labels) or the label without a string-file reader; `_global.InGame` is set true by the WindowManager (extern `InGame` answering "1" is verified at RotWK 0x815EF3 / 0x8154A5, the writer of the global is not); the retail AI levels are Easy 2, Medium 3, Hard 4, Brutal 5 and the human 6 (0x8419DB, 0x841A1B, 0x8009A7) | notes `skirmish-inferences`, `skirmish-random-unresolved` (at every StartGame message), `unported-gadget-init`, `unported-component` | `stops S-178 / S-179: ...`, `retail lobby input: the acceptance click-through ...` (unresolved list and note) and `skirmish setup: startPressed ...` (test_apt_skirmish.cpp) | a deterministic skirmish start; Not ported: the layer links and portals of the expansion (RW 0x6F6286, S-161). What RotWK's rules leave, as read: only the blocked unit patches (lower path priority, then lower id, RW 0x66D16E); the other walks on; horde members never collide (RW 0x66E233 returns for a horde member, RW 0x6939DF); a moving unit holds no position (RW 0x8E26B7), so a patch may lead through a moving enemy; the targets include raw nodes behind the unit. Measured (OpenBFME timings, 90 crossings of infantry hordes with move orders, test_move3_horde_spacing.cpp): deep enemy member pair-frames 4040 -> 3377, head-on runs with the horde objects closer than 60: 12 -> 3; after the IDLE-1 merge (update phases RW 0x851E97 / 0x490AC4, the hub's disabled-motion skip RW 0x874724) 3798 and 16. Lane MOVE-3 r2 read the member side: a horde member never reaches blockedBy (RW 0x66E233 returns for a horde member through RW 0x6939DF; the only collide interface with that entry is AIUpdate's, vtable RW 0xC10578), the member's own steps test the terrain only (RW 0x66C748 -> 0x6EA4E7 / 0x6F1B3E), its slot destination too (RW 0x6EA5B2), the hub's crowd search is unused (S-1832), PhysicsBehavior has no collide interface (S-782), HordeMemberCollide reacts in melee only (RW 0x8C0518), and RotWK's collision manager tests the horde objects' own shapes (a 30 x 45 box, S-780), which meet later than the port's bounding circles. So RotWK lets the members of crossing hostile hordes overlap: only the lower-priority horde object stops and patches its path. So by RotWK's rules as read, ElvenLorienArcherHorde and DwarvenAxeThrowerHorde (ranks at X 30 / 50) sent to one point together or one after the other share it (measured: 0.1 apart), while hordes with a rank near the object (GondorFighterHorde X 10) end 100 apart. A box turned by the goal's angle (the polygon fill, S-164) is not ported: hordes facing across their move would meet more member goals. | S-1923 | code | Windows crash dumps (WINCRASH-1) | Godot 4.7's Windows crash handler (the official MinGW export templates: msvcrt signal handlers + libbacktrace) symbolizes its own executable only, and only on the main thread (msvcrt keeps SIGSEGV / SIGILL / SIGFPE handlers per thread): an extension frame is `openbfme.windows.template_debug.x86_64.dll+<offset> - no debug info`, and a crash on any other thread writes no dump at all. An in-process symbolizer (GodotCrashReport, rounds 1-2) was removed in round 3: code running inside the fault could not be shown safe. The PDB is PRIVATE: every Windows build writes it (CodeView line tables, prefix-mapped, home prefixes scrubbed), tools/release/archive_symbols.sh keeps it only in the builder's local symbol store (it refuses a git work tree or a release folder), and the package audit and the commit gate refuse a .pdb or MSF bytes anywhere; an offset is mapped there with `llvm-symbolizer --obj=<dll> --relative-address 0x<offset>` | `RELEASE [S-1923] ...` printed into every Windows session log at start (scripts/release/release.gd), naming both limits | `test_the_session_log_names_stop_s1923_on_windows`, `test_debug_symbols_never_ship` (tools/release/test_pdb_tools.py) | a tester's crash log naming extension functions and lines directly; any report of a worker-thread crash | an out-of-process crash reporter / watchdog: a small helper process started with the game that, when the game dies, writes a minidump of it and symbolizes it with the private PDB on the developer side (no code in the crashing process) |

| S-380 | code | Apt calls without a function | a script call whose callee is not a function (a method a defined value lacks, any method of undefined / null, a named function no scope defines) calls nothing and returns undefined, as the retail interpreter does: RotWK CallMethod 0x00B1C220 / CallNamedMethodPop 0x00B1D3B0 (BFME2 0x00B08070 / 0x00B09200) resolve the member and skip a non-function silently, and nothing in them reaches the Apt log (the only ActionScript callers of the warning strings are the uncaught-exception reports RotWK 0x00B11574 / 0x00B1AB47). The player records the call as the note `call-without-function` (detail: the message and the clip the program runs on) instead of a script error; a name of the binary's native table this port does not implement (createTextField, removeTextField, loadVariables, getTextFormat: S-104; Object.registerClass: RotWK getter 0x00B22160, body 0x00AF1EB0, not read or ported, called by GuiTest and OnlineHome) stays a script error. An object or array value used as a callee (by method, by name or unnamed) is skipped the same way (RotWK 0x00B1AA5F pops the argc arguments and pushes undefined; an undefined receiver pops argc + 3 at 0x00B1C955 and pushes undefined; CallNamedMethodPop discards the result at 0x00B1D406); NewObject of a non-class stays a script error. The sibling order that makes InGameSideCommandBar's Button<i> call Button<i+1>.UpdateFrameState before it exists is the retail one (advance 0x00AF7030 -> frame controls 0x00B23540, frame actions 0x00B23850, sweep 0x00B0BC30; pool run 0x00AFA850; flush 0x00AF86A0) | note `call-without-function` | `player: siblings placed on one frame run their frame 0 actions in depth order*`, `stops S-380*` (test_apt_player.cpp, test_apt_interpreter.cpp: object / array callees and the operand counts), `retail shell walk*` (test_apt_shell_walk.cpp: every call of the shell walk pinned with its root cause), `retail MainMenu: with AptLevel0*`, the player corpus baseline | nothing in retail behaviour; a port gap that removes a function the movies expect now surfaces as a note, caught only by the pinned lists | none needed (retail behaviour); keep the pinned lists current |

Target facts: the SAS callbacks (RW 0x54F406 -> 0x54CF11 / 0x54CFD0 / 0x54D083) send both colours doubled when the map's multiplier exceeds 1 (RW 0x4AC97F, 0x54CF3B); defaultw3d.fxo lights classic materials per vertex (`saturate((sum(L.Color * N.L) * ColorDiffuse + AmbientLight * ColorAmbient + ColorEmissive) * vertex colour * 0.5)`, spec in oD1, pixel `tex * (oD0 + oD1) * 2`); the shaders compute it in gamma space and convert once (Godot lights and environment no longer touch W3D models). | S-720 | code | Network run-ahead | RotWK paces a network game by a packet router: RW 0x65DD0F gates a frame on a 200 ms period from QueryPerformanceFrequency (RW 0x65DBFC; 0xD9F608 = 5) and drops lag beyond 2 s; RW 0x8D3FD9 (BFME2's hasPacketRouterFrameStall) stalls the router only when a peer's latest frame (ConnectionManager + 0x12060) plus the slack is at or below the current frame, the slack being 3 before frame 6 and then GlobalData + 0xC18 = NetworkRunAheadSlack (10 in 2.01's GameData.ini); non-router peers advance on announced frames and complete command counts (RW 0x8D4E89, 0x8D77B5); RW 0x8D4301 / 0x8D3AF1 stamp unassigned commands with the current frame + 1; RotWK has no RUNAHEAD / RUNAHEADMETRICS command (RW 0x989449). The router protocol is not ported: OpenBFME keeps MP-1's symmetric lockstep, and lane MP-2's packet router adapts the common command delay to its measured round trip (Network::updateRunAhead: every NetworkRunAheadMetricsTime ms, ZH ConnectionManager::updateRunAhead's (latency / 2) * fps plus NetworkRunAheadSlack percent, the latency being the transport's smoothed round trip plus four times its variation (the transport's resend timeout backs off per RFC 6298 so slow links get samples), + 1 frame, at least the lobby's run-ahead, down one frame per period; sent as ZH's RUNAHEAD frame command, OpenBFME type 0x82, which every peer applies when its frame runs; a command issued on protocol frame L executes at L plus the run-ahead the frames before L agreed on (Network::runAheadAt), so the MSG_LOGIC_CRCs of all peers keep one execution frame however far each logic worker has acquired batches; only the frame's packet router's RUNAHEAD (the role passes on at a drop frame) within the lobby's run-ahead .. maxRunAhead (25) is taken, a fall of more than one frame is refused) | `Network::stopLines()` `[S-720]` (every peer report) | `net core: the network layer's acceptance stops ...` (test_net_core.cpp), `mp2 run-ahead: the packet router's measured round trip sets every peer's command delay ...` (test_mp2_disconnect.cpp), `mp2 net retail: on a slow link ...` (test_mp2_net_retail.cpp) | latency hiding equal to retail's router; cross-play | port the router: the frame-info publisher RW 0x8D0AA9, the received-command path RW 0x8D2F04 and the ceiling RW 0x8D34B4 |

| S-810 | code | Frame pacing: the logic worker's open limits | The logic runs on a worker thread (`LiveGame::Options::logicThread`, GameWorld option `logic_thread`, default on for `start_new_game`; off is the single-thread fallback), the one owner of the simulation while it runs a frame (frame / phase / object / module / command order and RNG unchanged). The logic tells its client through ordered data-only events (`GameClient/ClientEvents`, applied once in order; the drawables' client modules are resolved on the logic owner, `ModuleFactory::resolveClientModule`, so the render side never interns into the logic's name keys or counts in its factory), the drawables are posed from the immutable snapshot of the last completed frame (`GameClient/LogicSnapshot`). Review r2: no client code reads live objects while the worker runs a frame: the radar's blips (`Radar::blips(snapshot, ...)`) and the camera's lock / follow target (`TacticalCamera::setObjectSource`, `LiveGame::presentedObjectPosition`) come from the presented snapshot, the HUD's per-frame update is skipped and its input deferred (`InGameHudNode` replays it in order) in render frames the worker is busy in, and every bound query / command waits for the worker (`LiveGame::waitIdle`). Open: the HUD's selection, control bar and picking read the completed live state at those idle points instead of a snapshot. MP-1 integration (Sol, reviews r3 / r4, done): the frame driver (`LiveGameFrameDriver`) is split into a protocol-owner half (`pump`, `acquire`, `completed`) and a simulation-owner hook (`simulationAfterFrame`); the LockstepDriver, its Network, the Transport and the ReplayWriter have one owner, the thread that calls `LiveGame::advance`, which pumps the transport on every call (also `advance(0)`, while the worker runs and while the lockstep stalls), stamps the local input once against the protocol frame (the logic frame of the last completion consumed; `LockstepDriver::setInputSource` for input keyed to explicit protocol frames), acquires batch N only after the load barrier and once every needed slot's frame info and declared command count are in (zero-command frames included), each exactly once and in order, into a bounded FIFO (`kMaxQueuedBatches`, backpressure keeps the next batch with the driver). The worker checks N == the logic frame, installs the batch immediately before phase 1, runs the six phases once and publishes an immutable completion (frame N + 1, its hash, the CRC frame's breakdown, an RNG fold) with the snapshot; the protocol owner hands it to the driver in order (the replay's hash record N + 1, `Network::frameCompleted` from the captured breakdown, the CRC sent with execution frame N + 1 + run-ahead). `maxFramesPerAdvance` and a stall drop clock time only (`stalledFrames`), never a batch. `setFrameDriver` and `drainFrames` drain the queued complete batches (never a wait for the network) before a driver change, a leave, a replay's end or shutdown. The snapshot's local-player shroud read uses `ShroudManager::clientObjectStatus` (no cache write: `getObjectStatus`'s lazy status write is hashed and differed per peer). | `[S-810]` in `DrawableManager::Report::stops` (`RenderInterpolation::stopLines()`), carried by the `GameWorld` load report | `smooth1: the smooth-motion stops are reported ...` (test_smooth_interpolation.cpp), `smooth1 retail: the logic worker gives the single-thread game frame for frame ...` (test_smooth_threaded.cpp), `smooth1 retail: the render side's per-frame work ... runs beside the logic worker` (test_smooth_live.cpp; `tools/smooth/sanitize_threaded.sh tsan` for the race detector), `smooth1 r4 retail: lockstep batches on the logic worker ...` (test_smooth_net.cpp), `net retail smooth1: two peer processes with the logic worker ...` (test_net_retail.cpp, `tools/smooth/net_threaded.sh` under TSan / ASan) and the STOPS registration check (test_logic_objects.cpp) | HUD snapshot reads | snapshot fields for selection, health, production and picking |

The drawn pose itself is retail's (RW 0x6765B9: D3DXVec3CatmullRom over the previous, recorded, current and pending positions, RW 0xB27C80's slerp of the recorded towards the current transform, the stale snap when the last move is two frames old) from the snapshot RW 0x674B1F gathers in phase 1 (lane SMOOTH-2: the drawn position and the presented time go further, S-1140). Presentation only: the simulation, its rate and its hash are untouched. | `[S-811]` in `DrawableManager::Report::stops` (`RenderInterpolation::stopLines()`) | `smooth1: the smooth-motion stops are reported ...`, `smooth1: retail's drawn pose from a snapshot record ...` (test_smooth_interpolation.cpp) | how close the motion looks to retail at 30 fps | none needed for play; a retail capture of a moving horde at 30 fps compares the curve |

| S-831 | code | Gamma-space compositing | Retail (D3D9, no sRGB conversion) modulates textures and blends the frame buffer on the gamma-encoded values the display shows: white ALPHA at alpha 0.25 over black displays 0.25. Godot's Forward+ colour buffer is linear, which displayed 0.5333 (RENDER-3 review) and made the white construction dust (BuildingContructDust: RGB 255, Shader ALPHA, Alpha2 0.15..0.35) far too opaque. Ported (lane RENDER-3): GodotGammaComposite's two CompositorEffects on the active camera sRGB-encode the RGBA16F colour buffer before the transparent pass and decode it after; the transparent-pass shaders hand over gamma-space values (FX particles with raw texels x raw diffuse, streaks, blended W3D surfaces, roads / rivers / standing water at output_gamma 1); the opaque pass and the alpha-test particles keep one conversion; retail's colour and alpha data are unchanged. Measured: 0.2471 (the alpha byte 63). Review r2: with MSAA the encode rewrites every sample of the multisample attachment the transparent pass draws into (a copy read per sample by a sample-shaded full-screen pass; the decode stays on the resolved layer), so MSAA 2x / 4x keep an opaque grey 0.5 at 0.4980 and show white 0.25 over it as 0.6235 (previously 0.2157 / 0.4118); if that pass cannot run, ensure() turns the camera's viewport to MSAA off and reports why. MapTerrainBuilder's map root is a GammaCompositeHost and the road / river / standing water materials get output_gamma 1 when they are built (no longer from GameWorld's shroud refresh). NOT reproduced (reported, pinned): D3D9 saturates every blend result to [0, 1], the RGBA16F buffer does not, so additive overbright survives later alpha / multiply draws (particles, streaks, additive W3D overlaps): additive red + additive red + black at 0.5 shows (1, 0, 0), retail about (0.5, 0, 0); white + additive red + multiply grey 0.5 shows (1, 0.502, 0.502), retail about (0.5, 0.5, 0.5); a fix must saturate between draws (a clamp after the pass leaves both wrong). Review r3: the default MSAA encode reads the resolved opaque image (access_resolved_color) once per pixel and writes its gamma encoding to all samples (no attachment copy, no per-sample shading); interior pixels are exact, an opaque geometry EDGE keeps Godot's linear-space sample average (half-covered grey 0.8 over 0.5 shows 0.6706) where retail averages displayed values (0.65), reported as an S-831 line; the project setting openbfme/rendering/gamma_exact_msaa_edges selects the exact per-sample encode (shows 0.6471). Framebuffers, pipelines and sample copies are owned per attachment / format and released (two simultaneous 2x / 4x viewports create none in 600 frames). Review r4: a per-frame prune on the render thread (frame_post_draw -> call_on_render_thread, bound to the encode effect, disconnected at shutdown) retires orphaned entries whether or not an MSAA viewport remains: switching the exact mode off, turning MSAA off or deleting the last MSAA viewport frees the sample copies within a few frames (gamma_composite_test _case_retirement). Cost (AMD NAVI33, paused Evendim scene, 102 objects, GPU timestamps, 144-fps cap): review r2 with MSAA disabled, +0.185 ms/frame at 1440p and +0.997 ms/frame at 4K; review r3 with 4x MSAA and the per-sample encode, +2.10-2.11 ms/frame at 1440p and +4.23-4.25 ms/frame at 4K, total GPU time about 4.06-4.08 ms / 8.20-8.23 ms (4K / 4x missed the 6.944 ms budget for 144 fps). Lane RENDER-3 r3 (same scene, medians, the two modes measured back to back): default encode 2x +0.51 ms (1440p, total 2.17) / +1.40 ms (4K, total 4.81), 4x +0.60 ms (1440p, total 2.57) / +1.59 ms (4K, total 5.57: within the 144-fps budget); exact per-sample encode 2x +1.33 / +3.21 ms, 4x +2.38 / +4.90 ms (4K total 8.89). Busy-battle and Steam Deck APU performance are not established.
